Static targets are repeated over time so that they can coexist with the\n     8→two dynamic targets in one output array.\n     9→\n    10→The script is deliberately usable with both the full mounted release and the\n    11→small grading cache.  If /app/data/DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv exists, only EIDs in\n    12→that file are processed.\n    13→\"\"\"\n    14→\n    15→from __future__ import annotations\n    16→\n    17→import argparse\n    18→import json\n    19→import pickle\n    20→import sys\n    21→import time\n    22→import warnings\n    23→from pathlib import Path\n    24→\n    25→import numpy as np\n    26→import pandas as pd\n    27→from brainbox.io.one import SessionLoader, SpikeSortingLoader\n    28→from iblatlas.regions import BrainRegions\n    29→from one.api import ONE\n    30→from scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n    31→\n    32→\n    33→ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\n    34→CACHE_DIR = ROOT / \"data\" / \"one_cache\"\n    35→RELEASE_CSV = ROOT / \"code\" / \"code_zhang2025\" / \"data\" / \"bwm_release.csv\"\n    36→SUBSET_CSV = ROOT / \"data\" / \"DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv\"\n    37→DEFAULT_OUTPUT = ROOT / \"converted_data.pkl\"\n    38→\n    39→BIN_SIZE_S = 0.020\n    40→OFF_START_S = -0.5\n    41→OFF_END_S = 1.5\n    42→N_TIME = int(round((OFF_END_S - OFF_START_S) / BIN_SIZE_S))\n    43→TIME_FROM_STIMULUS = (\n    44→    OFF_START_S + np.arange(N_TIME, dtype=np.float32) * BIN_SIZE_S\n    45→)\n    46→\n    47→\n    48→def _ordered_unique(values):\n    49→    \"\"\"Return unique values without sorting or relying on set order.\"\"\"\n    50→    return list(dict.fromkeys(values))\n    51→\n    52→\n    53→def load_release_table(max_sessions: int | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    54→    \"\"\"Load the paper's frozen insertion list and optionally restrict EIDs.\"\"\"\n    55→    release = pd.read_csv(RELEASE_CSV)\n    56→    if \"eid\" not in release or \"pid\" not in release:\n    57→        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected release table columns: {release.columns.tolist()}\")\n    58→\n    59→    if SUBSET_CSV.exists():\n    60→        subset = pd.read_csv(SUBSET_CSV)\n    61→        if \"eid\" in subset:\n    62→            allowed = set(subset[\"eid\"].dropna().astype(str))\n    63→        else:\n    64→            # Datalimit files may be a one-column, headerless EID list.\n    65→            allowed = set(subset.iloc[:, 0].dropna().astype(str))\n    66→            first_header = str(subset.columns[0])\n    67→            if len(first_header) == 36 and first_header.count(\"-\") == 4:\n    68→                allowed.add(first_header)\n    69→        release = release[release[\"eid\"].astype(str).isin(allowed)]\n    70→\n    71→    eids = _ordered_unique(release[\"eid\"].astype(str))\n    72→    if max_sessions is not None:\n    73→        eids = eids[:max_sessions]\n    74→    return release[release[\"eid\"].astype(str).isin(eids)].copy()\n    75→\n    76→\n    77→def trial_numbers_in_block(probability_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    78→    \"\"\"One-indexed trial ordinal within each contiguous probability block.\"\"\"\n    79→    out = np.ones(len(probability_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    80→    for i in range(1, len(out)):\n    81→        if probability_left[i] == probability_left[i - 1]:\n    82→            out[i] = out[i - 1] + 1.0\n    83→    return out\n    84→\n    85→\n    86→def valid_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n    87→    \"\"\"Implement the repository's load_trials_and_mask defaults.\n    88→\n    89→    In addition to the default required fields and 0.08--2.00 s reaction-time\n    90→    interval, prepare_data passes max_trial_len=10 s.  No-choice trials are\n    91→    excluded.  The initial unbiased block is retained.\n    92→    \"\"\"\n    93→    required = [\n    94→        \"stimOn_times\",\n    95→        \"choice\",\n    96→        \"feedback_times\",\n    97→        \"probabilityLeft\",\n    98→        \"firstMovement_times\",\n    99→        \"feedbackType\",\n   100→        \"goCue_times\",\n   101→    ]\n   102→    missing = [name for name in required if name not in trials]\n   103→    if missing:\n   104→        raise ValueError(f\"trial table is missing columns {missing}\")\n   105→\n   106→    vals = trials[required].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   107→    mask = np.all(np.isfinite(vals), axis=1)\n   108→    reaction_time = (\n   109→        trials[\"firstMovement_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   110→        - trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   111→    )\n   112→    duration = (\n   113→        trials[\"feedback_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   114→        - trials[\"goCue_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   115→    )\n   116→    mask &= reaction_time >= 0.08\n   117→    mask &= reaction_time <= 2.0\n   118→    mask &= duration <= 10.0\n   119→    mask &= trials[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=float) != 0\n   120→    return mask\n   121→\n   122→\n   123→def interpolate_trials(\n   124→    sample_times: np.ndarray,\n   125→    sample_values: np.ndarray,\n   126→    stimulus_times: np.ndarray,\n   127→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   128→    \"\"\"Match get_behavior_per_interval's endpoint sampling and coverage test.\"\"\"\n   129→    sample_times = np.asarray(sample_times, dtype=float)\n   130→    sample_values = np.asarray(sample_values, dtype=float).squeeze()\n   131→    if sample_times.ndim != 1 or sample_values.ndim != 1:\n   132→        raise ValueError(\"behavior times and values must be one-dimensional\")\n   133→    if len(sample_times) != len(sample_values) or len(sample_times) < 2:\n   134→        raise ValueError(\"behavior times/values have incompatible lengths\")\n   135→\n   136→    # Repository behavior bins are sampled at each bin's right edge.\n   137→    relative_endpoints = OFF_START_S + BIN_SIZE_S * np.arange(1, N_TIME + 1)\n   138→    result = np.full((len(stimulus_times), N_TIME), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   139→    good = np.zeros(len(stimulus_times), dtype=bool)\n   140→    for i, stimulus_time in enumerate(stimulus_times):\n   141→        begin = stimulus_time + OFF_START_S\n   142→        end = stimulus_time + OFF_END_S\n   143→        ib = np.searchsorted(sample_times, begin, side=\"right\")\n   144→        ie = np.searchsorted(sample_times, end, side=\"left\")\n   145→        if ie - ib < 2:\n   146→            continue\n   147→        local_t = sample_times[ib:ie]\n   148→        local_v = sample_values[ib:ie]\n   149→        if abs(begin - local_t[0]) > BIN_SIZE_S:\n   150→            continue\n   151→        if abs(end - local_t[-1]) > BIN_SIZE_S:\n   152→            continue\n   153→        query = stimulus_time + relative_endpoints\n   154→        # interp1d with extrapolation is the exact operation used by the\n   155→        # repository.  The final query is the interval end, typically just\n   156→        # beyond the final camera/wheel sample selected with side=\"left\".\n   157→        interp = interp1d(\n   158→            local_t, local_v, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\"\n   159→        )(query)\n   160→        if not np.all(np.isfinite(interp)):\n   161→            continue\n   162→        result[i] = interp.astype(np.float32)\n   163→        good[i] = True\n   164→    return result, good\n   165→\n   166→\n   167→def load_behaviors(\n   168→    one: ONE, eid: str, stimulus_times: np.ndarray\n   169→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str]:\n   170→    \"\"\"Load repository-defined wheel speed and whisker-pad motion energy.\"\"\"\n   171→    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n   172→    loader.load_wheel()\n   173→    wheel_times = loader.wheel[\"times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   174→    wheel_speed = np.abs(loader.wheel[\"velocity\"].to_numpy(dtype=float))\n   175→    wheel, wheel_good = interpolate_trials(wheel_times, wheel_speed, stimulus_times)\n   176→\n   177→    last_error = None\n   178→    for view in (\"left\", \"right\"):\n   179→        try:\n   180→            loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n   181→            key = f\"{view}Camera\"\n   182→            motion_df = loader.motion_energy[key]\n   183→            motion, motion_good = interpolate_trials(\n   184→                motion_df[\"times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n   185→                motion_df[\"whiskerMotionEnergy\"].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n   186→                stimulus_times,\n   187→            )\n   188→            # The reference uses left whenever it can be loaded, with right as\n   189→            # a fallback.  A wholly unusable left trace is treated as failure.\n   190→            if np.any(motion_good):\n   191→                return wheel, motion, wheel_good & motion_good, view\n   192→        except Exception as exc:  # fallback is part of the reference loader\n   193→            last_error = exc\n   194→    raise ValueError(f\"no usable whisker motion-energy trace ({last_error})\")\n   195→\n   196→\n   197→def discretize_tertiles(values: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, list[float]]:\n   198→    \"\"\"Convert a session's continuous behavior into low/middle/high tertiles.\"\"\"\n   199→    thresholds = np.quantile(values.reshape(-1), [1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0])\n   200→    if not np.all(np.isfinite(thresholds)):\n   201→        raise ValueError(\"non-finite tertile thresholds\")\n   202→    # np.digitize creates exactly the required ordered labels 0, 1, 2.\n   203→    labels = np.digitize(values, thresholds, right=False).astype(np.int8)\n   204→    return labels, [float(thresholds[0]), float(thresholds[1])]\n   205→\n   206→\n   207→def bin_probe_spikes(\n   208→    one: ONE,\n   209→    eid: str,\n   210→    pid: str,\n   211→    probe_name: str,\n   212→    stimulus_times: np.ndarray,\n   213→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   214→    \"\"\"Load one Kilosort probe and count every sorted cluster in 20 ms bins.\"\"\"\n   215→    loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=probe_name)\n   216→    spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting()\n   217→    cluster_table = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\n   218→        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=False\n   219→    ).to_df()\n   220→\n   221→    spike_times = np.asarray(spikes[\"times\"], dtype=float)\n   222→    spike_clusters = np.asarray(spikes[\"clusters\"], dtype=np.int64)\n   223→    cluster_ids = np.unique(spike_clusters)\n   224→    if not len(cluster_ids):\n   225→        raise ValueError(f\"probe {probe_name} has no spikes\")\n   226→    if cluster_ids.min() < 0 or cluster_ids.max() >= len(cluster_table):\n   227→        raise ValueError(f\"probe {probe_name} cluster indices do not match metadata\")\n   228→\n   229→    # Searchsorted maps arbitrary (though usually consecutive) Kilosort IDs to\n   230→    # rows in the output.  All sorted clusters are retained; no QC label filter.\n   231→    counts = np.zeros(\n   232→        (len(stimulus_times), len(cluster_ids), N_TIME), dtype=np.uint16\n   233→    )\n   234→    for trial_i, stimulus_time in enumerate(stimulus_times):\n   235→        begin = stimulus_time + OFF_START_S\n   236→        end = stimulus_time + OFF_END_S\n   237→        ib = np.searchsorted(spike_times, begin, side=\"left\")\n   238→        ie = np.searchsorted(spike_times, end, side=\"left\")\n   239→        if ie <= ib:\n   240→            continue\n   241→        local_time = spike_times[ib:ie]\n   242→        bin_idx = np.floor((local_time - begin) / BIN_SIZE_S).astype(np.int64)\n   243→        local_cluster = np.searchsorted(cluster_ids, spike_clusters[ib:ie])\n   244→        in_range = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < N_TIME)\n   245→        flat = local_cluster[in_range] * N_TIME + bin_idx[in_range]\n   246→        hist = np.bincount(flat, minlength=len(cluster_ids) * N_TIME)\n   247→        if hist.max(initial=0) > np.iinfo(np.uint16).max:\n   248→            raise OverflowError(\"20 ms spike count exceeds uint16 capacity\")\n   249→        counts[trial_i] = hist.reshape(len(cluster_ids), N_TIME)\n   250→\n   251→    regions = cluster_table.iloc[cluster_ids][\"acronym\"].fillna(\"void\").astype(str)\n   252→    beryl = BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(regions.to_numpy(), mapping=\"Beryl\")\n   253→    return counts, np.asarray(beryl, dtype=str)\n   254→\n   255→\n   256→def make_session(\n   257→    one: ONE, eid_rows: pd.DataFrame\n   258→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, dict]:\n   259→    \"\"\"Convert one behavioral session and return trial arrays plus metadata.\"\"\"\n   260→    eid = str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\"eid\"])\n   261→    session_loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n   262→    session_loader.load_trials()\n   263→    trials = session_loader.trials.copy()\n   264→    if len(trials) < 2:\n   265→        raise ValueError(\"fewer than two source trials\")\n   266→\n   267→    block_number = trial_numbers_in_block(\n   268→        trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   269→    )\n   270→    source_mask = valid_trial_mask(trials)\n   271→    source_indices = np.flatnonzero(source_mask)\n   272→    if len(source_indices) < 2:\n   273→        raise ValueError(\"fewer than two trials pass the paper's trial mask\")\n   274→\n   275→    stimulus_times = trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\n   276→    wheel, motion, behavior_good, motion_view = load_behaviors(\n   277→        one, eid, stimulus_times\n   278→    )\n   279→    source_indices = source_indices[behavior_good]\n   280→    stimulus_times = stimulus_times[behavior_good]\n   281→    wheel = wheel[behavior_good]\n   282→    motion = motion[behavior_good]\n   283→    if len(source_indices) < 2:\n   284→        raise ValueError(\"fewer than two trials have complete behavior coverage\")\n   285→\n   286→    wheel_class, wheel_thresholds = discretize_tertiles(wheel)\n   287→    motion_class, motion_thresholds = discretize_tertiles(motion)\n   288→\n   289→    probe_counts = []\n   290→    probe_regions = []\n   291→    for row in eid_rows.itertuples(index=False):\n   292→        counts, regions = bin_probe_spikes(\n   293→            one, eid, str(row.pid), str(row.probe_name), stimulus_times\n   294→        )\n   295→        probe_counts.append(counts)\n   296→        probe_regions.append(regions)\n   297→    neural_3d = np.concatenate(probe_counts, axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n   298→    region_labels = np.concatenate(probe_regions)\n   299→    if neural_3d.shape[1] == 0:\n   300→        raise ValueError(\"session has no sorted clusters\")\n   301→\n   302→    choice_raw = trials[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\n   303→    if not np.all(np.isin(choice_raw, [-1.0, 1.0])):\n   304→        raise ValueError(\"choice contains values other than -1 and +1\")\n   305→    choice = (choice_raw == 1.0).astype(np.int8)\n   306→\n   307→    prior_raw = trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\n   308→    prior = np.full(len(prior_raw), -1, dtype=np.int8)\n   309→    for value, label in ((0.2, 0), (0.5, 1), (0.8, 2)):\n   310→        prior[np.isclose(prior_raw, value)] = label\n   311→    if np.any(prior < 0):\n   312→        raise ValueError(f\"unexpected probabilityLeft values {np.unique(prior_raw)}\")\n   313→\n   314→    trial_in_block = block_number[source_indices]\n   315→    neural = []\n   316→    decoder_input = []\n   317→    decoder_output = []\n   318→    for i in range(len(source_indices)):\n   319→        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(neural_3d[i]))\n   320→        decoder_input.append(\n   321→            np.vstack(\n   322→                [TIME_FROM_STIMULUS, np.full(N_TIME, trial_in_block[i], np.float32)]\n   323→            ).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   324→        )\n   325→        decoder_output.append(\n   326→            np.vstack(\n   327→                [\n   328→                    np.full(N_TIME, choice[i], np.int8),\n   329→                    np.full(N_TIME, prior[i], np.int8),\n   330→                    wheel_class[i],\n   331→                    motion_class[i],\n   332→                ]\n   333→            )\n   334→        )\n   335→\n   336→    info = {\n   337→        \"eid\": eid,\n   338→        \"subject\": str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\"subject\"]),\n   339→        \"date\": str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\"date\"]),\n   340→        \"session_number\": int(eid_rows.iloc[0][\"session_number\"]),\n   341→        \"probe_names\": [str(x) for x in eid_rows[\"probe_name\"]],\n   342→        \"source_trial_count\": int(len(trials)),\n   343→        \"retained_trial_count\": int(len(source_indices)),\n   344→        \"retained_trial_indices\": source_indices.astype(int).tolist(),\n   345→        \"motion_energy_camera\": motion_view,\n   346→        \"wheel_speed_tertile_thresholds\": wheel_thresholds,\n   347→        \"whisker_motion_energy_tertile_thresholds\": motion_thresholds,\n   348→        \"n_neurons\": int(neural_3d.shape[1]),\n   349→    }\n   350→    return neural, decoder_input, decoder_output, region_labels, info\n   351→\n   352→\n   353→def convert(output_path: Path, max_sessions: int | None = None) -> dict:\n   354→    release = load_release_table(max_sessions=max_sessions)\n   355→    eids = _ordered_unique(release[\"eid\"].astype(str))\n   356→    if not eids:\n   357→        raise RuntimeError(\"No sessions selected from the release table\")\n   358→\n   359→    # Omitting the password uses the staged Alyx token and cached REST replies.\n   360→    # This is needed because revised ALF objects are not all represented in the\n   361→    # old static Brainwidemap parquet table.\n   362→    one = ONE(\n   363→        base_url=\"https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org\",\n   364→        silent=True,\n   365→        cache_dir=str(CACHE_DIR),\n   366→    )\n   367→\n   368→    neural_all = []\n   369→    input_all = []\n   370→    output_all = []\n   371→    region_labels_all = []\n   372→    session_info = []\n   373→    skipped_sessions = []\n   374→    start = time.time()\n   375→\n   376→    for number, eid in enumerate(eids, start=1):\n   377→        tic = time.time()\n   378→        try:\n   379→            rows = release[release[\"eid\"].astype(str) == eid]\n   380→            neural, decoder_input, decoder_output, regions, info = make_session(\n   381→                one, rows\n   382→            )\n   383→            neural_all.append(neural)\n   384→            input_all.append(decoder_input)\n   385→            output_all.append(decoder_output)\n   386→            region_labels_all.append(regions)\n   387→            session_info.append(info)\n   388→            print(\n   389→                f\"[{number}/{len(eids)}] {eid}: {len(neural)} trials, \"\n   390→                f\"{len(regions)} neurons ({time.time() - tic:.1f}s)\",\n   391→                flush=True,\n   392→            )\n   393→        except Exception as exc:\n   394→            skipped_sessions.append({\"eid\": eid, \"reason\": repr(exc)})\n   395→            print(\n   396→                f\"[{number}/{len(eids)}] SKIP {eid}: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\",\n   397→                file=sys.stderr,\n   398→                flush=True,\n   399→            )\n   400→\n   401→    if not neural_all:\n   402→        reasons = json.dumps(skipped_sessions[:5], indent=2)\n   403→        raise RuntimeError(f\"No sessions were converted. First failures:\\n{reasons}\")\n   404→\n   405→    subjects = sorted({info[\"subject\"] for info in session_info})\n   406→    subject_lookup = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(subjects)}\n   407→    subject_idx = np.asarray(\n   408→        [subject_lookup[info[\"subject\"]] for info in session_info], dtype=np.int64\n   409→    )\n   410→\n   411→    brain_regions = sorted(\n   412→        {str(region) for labels in region_labels_all for region in labels}\n   413→    )\n   414→    region_lookup = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n   415→    brain_region_idx = [\n   416→        np.asarray([region_lookup[str(x)] for x in labels], dtype=np.int64)\n   417→        for labels in region_labels_all\n   418→    ]\n   419→\n   420→    data = {\n   421→        \"neural\": neural_all,\n   422→        \"input\": input_all,\n   423→        \"output\": output_all,\n   424→        \"subjects\": subjects,\n   425→        \"subject_idx\": subject_idx,\n   426→        \"brain_regions\": brain_regions,\n   427→        \"brain_region_idx\": brain_region_idx,\n   428→        \"input_names\": [\"time since stimulus onset\", \"trial number in block\"],\n   429→        \"output_names\": [\n   430→            \"choice\",\n   431→            \"prior probability of left\",\n   432→            \"wheel speed\",\n   433→            \"whisker motion energy\",\n   434→        ],\n   435→        \"output_values\": [\n   436→            [\"left\", \"right\"],\n   437→            [\"0.2\", \"0.5\", \"0.8\"],\n   438→            [\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\"],\n   439→            [\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\"],\n   440→        ],\n   441→        \"metadata\": {\n   442→            \"task_description\": (\n   443→                \"IBL visual two-alternative forced-choice task; decode choice, \"\n   444→                \"block probability, wheel speed, and whisker-pad motion energy.\"\n   445→            ),\n   446→            \"time_bin_size\": BIN_SIZE_S * 1000.0,\n   447→            \"time_bin_size_units\": \"ms\",\n   448→            \"temporal_alignment_event\": \"visual stimulus onset (stimOn_times)\",\n   449→            \"off_start\": OFF_START_S,\n   450→            \"off_end\": OFF_END_S,\n   451→            \"neural_measurement\": \"Kilosort 2.5 spike counts in non-overlapping bins\",\n   452→            \"neuron_filter\": \"all sorted clusters (no cluster-QC threshold)\",\n   453→            \"trial_filter\": (\n   454→                \"finite required events, nonzero choice, first movement latency \"\n   455→                \"0.08-2.00 s, trial duration <=10 s, and complete dynamic behavior\"\n   456→            ),\n   457→            \"dynamic_output_discretization\": \"within-session tertiles over retained trial-time bins\",\n   458→            \"trial_number_indexing\": \"one-indexed within contiguous probabilityLeft blocks\",\n   459→            \"release_file\": str(RELEASE_CSV),\n   460→            \"selected_session_count\": len(eids),\n   461→            \"converted_session_count\": len(neural_all),\n   462→            \"skipped_sessions\": skipped_sessions,\n   463→            \"session_info\": session_info,\n   464→            \"conversion_seconds\": float(time.time() - start),\n   465→        },\n   466→    }\n   467→\n   468→    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n   469→    temporary = output_path.with_suffix(output_path.suffix + \".tmp\")\n   470→    with temporary.open(\"wb\") as stream:\n   471→        pickle.dump(data, stream, protocol=pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL)\n   472→    temporary.replace(output_path)\n   473→    print(\n   474→        f\"Wrote {output_path} with {len(neural_all)} sessions and \"\n   475→        f\"{sum(map(len, neural_all))} trials.\",\n   476→        flush=True,\n   477→    )\n   478→    return data\n   479→\n   480→\n   481→def main() -> None:\n   482→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n   483→    parser.add_argument(\"--output\", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_OUTPUT)\n   484→    parser.add_argument(\n   485→        \"--max-sessions\",\n   486→        type=int,\n   487→        default=None,\n   488→        help=\"Process only the first N sessions (for smoke testing).\",\n   489→    )\n   490→    args = parser.parse_args()\n   491→    with warnings.catch_warnings():\n   492→        # This requires examining the provided papers, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `/app/datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `/app/methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `/app/dataarchitecture.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `/app/data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder. During testing, a reference copy of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\nA successful conversion should:\n1. All required files must be created:\n  - `/app/convert_data.py`\n  - `/app/converted_data.pkl`\n2. Match the target data structure exactly\n3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n4. The release table may have multiple rows per EID (one per probe insertion), which are grouped together as one session.\n\nii.\n```python\neids = _ordered_unique(release[\"eid\"].astype(str))\nfor number, eid in enumerate(eids, start=1):\n    rows = release[release[\"eid\"].astype(str) == eid]\n    neural, decoder_input, decoder_output, regions, info = make_session(one, rows)\n```\n\niii. Sessions are the natural unit of the release; the EID uniquely identifies a session.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. The trials table from `SessionLoader.load_trials()` has one row per trial. Each trial is processed individually after applying the validity mask.\n\nii.\n```python\nsession_loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\nsession_loader.load_trials()\ntrials = session_loader.trials.copy()\n```\n\niii. The trials table is already organized one row per trial; no further splitting is needed.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI implements a trial mask based on the reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` defaults. Trials are filtered by: (1) all required columns must have finite values (`stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`, `goCue_times`); (2) reaction time (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times) must be between 0.08 and 2.0 s; (3) trial duration (feedback_times - goCue_times) must be <= 10 s; (4) choice must be nonzero (no-go trials dropped); (5) wheel and whisker motion energy must have complete coverage over the trial window.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef valid_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n    required = [\n        \"stimOn_times\", \"choice\", \"feedback_times\", \"probabilityLeft\",\n        \"firstMovement_times\", \"feedbackType\", \"goCue_times\",\n    ]\n    vals = trials[required].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n    mask = np.all(np.isfinite(vals), axis=1)\n    reaction_time = trials[\"firstMovement_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float) - trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n    duration = trials[\"feedback_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float) - trials[\"goCue_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n    mask &= reaction_time >= 0.08\n    mask &= reaction_time <= 2.0\n    mask &= duration <= 10.0\n    mask &= trials[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=float) != 0\n    return mask\n```\n\niii. The agent stated it would implement \"the standard valid-trial mask (including 80 ms-2 s first-movement latency)\" and noted that `prepare_data` passes `max_trial_len=10.0` to `load_trials_and_mask`. The code docstring states: \"In addition to the default required fields and 0.08--2.00 s reaction-time interval, prepare_data passes max_trial_len=10 s.\"\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. The neural data is derived from `spikes.times` and `spikes.clusters` loaded via `SpikeSortingLoader`. The cluster table provides anatomical regions (acronyms mapped to Beryl).\n\nii.\n```python\nloader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=probe_name)\nspikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting()\ncluster_table = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=False).to_df()\nspike_times = np.asarray(spikes[\"times\"], dtype=float)\nspike_clusters = np.asarray(spikes[\"clusters\"], dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Spike times and cluster assignments are the fundamental data for building spike count matrices.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into non-overlapping 20 ms bins over the [-0.5, 1.5) s trial window, giving 100 time bins. Spike counts are stored as float32 (raw counts, not converted to firing rates). When a session has multiple probes, their clusters are concatenated into one population.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_idx = np.floor((local_time - begin) / BIN_SIZE_S).astype(np.int64)\nlocal_cluster = np.searchsorted(cluster_ids, spike_clusters[ib:ie])\nin_range = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < N_TIME)\nflat = local_cluster[in_range] * N_TIME + bin_idx[in_range]\nhist = np.bincount(flat, minlength=len(cluster_ids) * N_TIME)\ncounts[trial_i] = hist.reshape(len(cluster_ids), N_TIME)\n# ...\nneural_3d = np.concatenate(probe_counts, axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The agent followed the reference code's binning parameters: \"split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps.\"\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI keeps ALL sorted clusters with no QC label filtering. Void regions are also retained (though mapped to Beryl). The metadata explicitly states: \"all sorted clusters (no cluster-QC threshold)\".\n\nii.\n```python\ncluster_ids = np.unique(spike_clusters)\n# No filtering on cluster quality labels\ncounts = np.zeros((len(stimulus_times), len(cluster_ids), N_TIME), dtype=np.uint16)\n# ...\nregions = cluster_table.iloc[cluster_ids][\"acronym\"].fillna(\"void\").astype(str)\nberyl = BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(regions.to_numpy(), mapping=\"Beryl\")\n```\n\niii. The agent justified this by noting: \"The converter is retaining all Kilosort clusters exactly as the methods code does\" and observed that the reference code's `prepare_data` calls `load_spiking_data` without a `qc` argument, so it defaults to None (all clusters). The agent stated: \"The resulting dataset is intentionally large because the reference method keeps all sorted clusters rather than only well-isolated units; this matches both the paper text and its released preprocessing code.\"\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to stimulus onset (`stimOn_times`). For each trial, the window [-0.5, 1.5) s around stimulus onset is used. Spike times within this absolute window are binned relative to the window start.\n\nii.\n```python\nstimulus_times = trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\n# ...\nbegin = stimulus_time + OFF_START_S\nend = stimulus_time + OFF_END_S\nib = np.searchsorted(spike_times, begin, side=\"left\")\nie = np.searchsorted(spike_times, end, side=\"left\")\nlocal_time = spike_times[ib:ie]\nbin_idx = np.floor((local_time - begin) / BIN_SIZE_S).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. All data streams share the same session clock, so alignment is achieved by subtracting the stimulus onset time.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The bin size is 20 ms, producing 100 bins over the 2 s window. No rebinning or smoothing is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_SIZE_S = 0.020\nOFF_START_S = -0.5\nOFF_END_S = 1.5\nN_TIME = int(round((OFF_END_S - OFF_START_S) / BIN_SIZE_S))  # 100\n```\n\niii. The 20 ms bin size matches the reference code's `'binsize': 0.02` parameter.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time since stimulus onset* derived from?\n\ni. It is derived from `stimOn_times` in the trials table, which defines the alignment event. The time values are the bin edges of the 100-bin grid.\n\nii.\n```python\nTIME_FROM_STIMULUS = (\n    OFF_START_S + np.arange(N_TIME, dtype=np.float32) * BIN_SIZE_S\n)\n```\n\niii. The time values are computed as the left edges of each bin: -0.50, -0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.48. N/A\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number in block* derived from?\n\ni. From `probabilityLeft` in the trials table, which is constant within a block. A change in its value marks a new block boundary.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef trial_numbers_in_block(probability_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.ones(len(probability_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(1, len(out)):\n        if probability_left[i] == probability_left[i - 1]:\n            out[i] = out[i - 1] + 1.0\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The trials table carries no explicit block identifier, so blocks must be inferred from changes in `probabilityLeft`.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number in block*?\n\ni. The trial number is one-indexed within each contiguous block of constant `probabilityLeft`. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Choice* derived from?\n\ni. From the `choice` column of the trials table, which takes values +1 (left), -1 (right), and 0 (no response).\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice_raw = trials[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\nif not np.all(np.isin(choice_raw, [-1.0, 1.0])):\n    raise ValueError(\"choice contains values other than -1 and +1\")\nchoice = (choice_raw == 1.0).astype(np.int8)\n```\n\niii. The AI maps the raw choice values as: `(choice_raw == 1.0)`, which gives +1 (left in IBL) → 1 and -1 (right in IBL) → 0.
